“Will I be alone forever?”

?hook=cards-alone-forever

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO FATE, IN ANY FORM — 'you are meant to be alone' is the most harmful sentence available on this funnel, and 'it is meant to be', 'the universe has a plan', 'this is preparing you', 'there is a reason for this' are the same sentence in kinder clothes: they make her suffering purposeful and assign it to her. 🔴 NO LIFE SENTENCE and 🔴 NO PROMISE — 'you will always be alone' and 'you will meet someone' are both strangers ruling on the rest of her life. 🔴 No pathologising: nothing about her being negative, bitter, closed off, self-sabotaging, or 'attracting what she puts out'. 🔴 BOTH ANSWERS ARE FORBIDDEN, so every card must VISIBLY DECLINE the absolute — a quiet sidestep reads as evasion rather than as a reading. The finding that replaces the verdict: 'forever' is not a length. It is what the WEIGHT feels like from underneath, and the weight is the real thing to name.

return-mhf she turned a face-down card

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — his white inner robe shows at the neck and the wrists.17w
  2. 2You asked me about alone and forever. Your hand went to the man still at work.16w
  3. 3So I will not tell you how long, dear. Nobody honestly can.12w
  4. 4And there's no clock anywhere on his table, dear.9w
  5. 5But you said forever, dear, and I don't think you meant years.12w
  6. 6That's why it lands so heavy, dear. Forever is the weight of it.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has made this so heavy to carry…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of hands that are still working, whatever the hour.
  2. 2Of the three you might have drawn, you took the one about ongoing labour, and labour is quietly what this has become for you.
  3. 3Nobody's forever exists anywhere a card could read it, and I would not hand you one even if it did — what the Magician carries here is the weight rather than the length. You have begun saying 'forever' because that is the word exhaustion reaches for once it has carried something a long while without relief. It is not a prediction you have made, and it is not you being defeatist. It is an accurate report of how heavy this has got, and those two things are mistaken for one another constantly, usually by people who have not had to carry it.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what this has actually been weighing…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — nothing is holding him up but one rope at the ankle.18w
  2. 2You asked me about alone and forever. Your hand went to the card that keeps no clock.17w
  3. 3So this card never measures time, dear. It only hangs there.11w
  4. 4And there's no clock on it anywhere, dear. None at all.11w
  5. 5But you're not counting years, dear. You're tired.8w
  6. 6That's why the days feel long, dear. You've carried this on your own.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been adding to the load…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of time that has stopped moving at its usual speed.
  2. 2It was the card of the slowed hour your fingers settled on, and slowness has been doing a great deal of the damage.
  3. 3Length is the one thing the Hanged Man never measures, and no honest reading measures it either. What it registers is that your sense of time has been altered by going without — a long enough stretch rearranges how anything feels, until a year of it and the whole rest of a life become indistinguishable from the inside. None of that is a defect in how you think. It is what endurance does to a clock, and you have been judging your entire future from a measurement taken while tired.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the measurement you have been using…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the bundle on his stick is smaller than his head.16w
  2. 2You asked me about alone and forever. Your hand went to the one who packs light.16w
  3. 3So forever cannot be made to hold, dear. Not by me.11w
  4. 4And he carries little, dear, because the far end is unknown.11w
  5. 5But what I can see is the weight you've been under, dear.12w
  6. 6That's why you are worn out, dear. I can see that much.12w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been pressing down on you…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of ground that has not been walked on yet.
  2. 2Under a question about always, up came the card that knows nothing whatever of always.
  3. 3The Fool holds no forever and cannot be made to hold one; I will not tell you this ends and I will not tell you it does not, because neither sentence would be worth the breath it took. What sits in it instead is that 'forever' is a shape the mind puts around a thing while that thing is still happening, and nobody has ever managed to see out of the middle of something. You are not being asked to believe anything cheerful here. You are being told that the view from where you are standing is not the view.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the view you have been taking for the whole of it…

“Will we get back together?”

?hook=cards-back-together

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). The US-FRAMED sibling of `reunion`, and that pairing is the whole experiment — reunion casts him as the agent and her as the one waiting; these cast the relationship as a joint thing with two people in it. 🔴 THE FORBIDDEN VERDICT IS ON THE RELATIONSHIP, NOT ON HIM — harder, not softer. A claim about a man can at least be measured against his behaviour; 'it is over' or 'it is not over' is unfalsifiable to her and lands as a ruling on her life from someone who has never met either of them. 🔴 NO ODDS — cards-still-a-chance asks for a number outright and there is none; no percentage, no 'strong chance', no 'likely'. 🔴 NO DIRECTIVES in either direction: move on, let go, fight for him, reach out first are all out. 🔴 Never hand her the outcome ('it is up to you', 'if you had only') — the us-framing makes that easy and it lands as blame. Finding, and the one that is truest to the us-framing: this question is about a JOINT thing and only one of the two people is in the room. She has been carrying a two-person decision on her own — which is why it will not resolve however long she turns it over.

return-mhf she turned a face-down card

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — his right arm is raised higher than the top of his head.18w
  2. 2You came asking about getting back together. Your hand went to the man working alone.15w
  3. 3So this isn't finished, dear. The card won't let me call it that.13w
  4. 4And on that table, dear, one pair of hands does all the work.13w
  5. 5But a thing like this takes two, dear, and only you have been working it.15w
  6. 6That's why nothing moves, however hard you push. You've been the only one pushing.14w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been keeping this a one-person job…14w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the thing that only exists if two people build it.
  2. 2Your hand went to the card of what has to be made rather than waited for, and for a question worded the way yours is, that is worth noticing.
  3. 3The Magician holds no picture of the two of you a year from now — what it marks is that the thing you are asking about was never a single event you could watch for. It is two separate decisions that have to meet, and you have been treating it as one outcome to be granted to you. Half of it has always been yours, and you are allowed to know what your own half is well before his ever arrives.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the half of this that has always been yours…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — his tied leg is straight, and the rope is short.17w
  2. 2Your question was about getting back together. Your hand went to the card that holds the middle.17w
  3. 3So you've been living in the middle of this, dear. It has no floor.14w
  4. 4And look at him. Neither down nor up, and quite still.11w
  5. 5But the middle is a place two people leave together, dear.11w
  6. 6That's why you keep nearly getting out, dear, and end up back in it.14w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been holding you in the middle…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of what was left standing in the doorway, neither in nor out.
  2. 2You reached for the card of the thing never closed and never resumed, which is the exact place the two of you have been left.
  3. 3The Hanged Man is not in the business of telling you how this ends, and I will not put words in its mouth — what it shows is a thing suspended rather than finished, and two people can be held in a suspension without either of them having chosen it. That is not the same as being over, and it is not the same as being on its way back. What it has cost you is having to live inside it while nobody will name it.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what has been left standing between you…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the stick over his shoulder is long and thin.15w
  2. 2You asked about the road back together. Your hand went to the one who set out alone.17w
  3. 3So the road back is still there, dear. Nobody has closed it.12w
  4. 4And look at him. One road, one traveller, no second pair of boots.13w
  5. 5But a road back needs two people walking it, dear.10w
  6. 6That's why rest doesn't fix it, dear. You walked both parts of this.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been standing on the road back…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the road that could still be walked from either end.
  2. 2That is not random; you reached for the card of the beginning, and a beginning is the one thing that cannot be inherited from what came before it.
  3. 3The Fool carries no map of where the two of you end up — what it points at is that whatever opens here would be a different thing wearing a familiar name, not the old one handed back to you intact. You would not be collecting something you left behind. You would be deciding, with the same person and with everything you now know, whether to make something else — and that is a choice you are entitled to make deliberately rather than drift into.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what would actually be beginning…

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Money translation: there is no man here, so cut 4 becomes what the block has been DOING, cut 5 becomes the EARNING against the KEEPING, and cut 6 names the near-miss she has lived — the VOC's own sentence. 🔴 NONE of the seven bans below was loosened with the love families; this is the one place where a wrong sentence costs her actual money. 🆕 TRACK B — the first NON-LOVE family on /fb-tarot. Face-down return-mhf, no new art. 🔴 SEVEN BANS, three of which exist nowhere else on the funnel. No amount, no date, no SOURCE (no inheritance, no windfall, no legal case — she can act on an invented source with her actual savings). 🔴🔴 Never name a person as the block: the accusation lands on someone real inside a real family. 🔴🔴 No financial advice in any form — invest, sell, hold, take the pension, delay it, go back to work, stop working. This is the one ban where the harm is measured in her money. 🔴🔴 Never blame her: no poverty mindset, no 'you attract lack', no vibration. 🔴 Never 'too late' and never a promised arrival. 🔴 Never presume the state of her finances — she said blocked, not broke. ✅ GUARDED as of 2026-08-19: `tests/tarot-money-block-copy.test.ts` now exists and runs against these drafts DIRECTLY. It had to: the drafts setup can only patch a hook the registry already has, so an unwired family is checked by nothing at all and still prints a green tick. All seven bans were fed a deliberate violation and all seven fired. 📊 WHAT THE VOC CHANGED. These hooks have never run, so there is no per-hook data. The nearest real corpus is docs/v1-money-bucket-voc.md — 10,514 V1 money concerns. Two themes matter here and they pull in different directions. **Retirement (554 concerns, the highest earner in the bucket at $15,146/1k):** the emotion is not panic, it is quiet arithmetic dread. 'I'm worried that I'll outlive my few savings.' 'Prices are going up but my pension is not.' 'I thought I would be set by now.' **The invisible block (1,208 concerns) — and its instruction is explicit: 'The near-miss is the hook, not the poverty.'** 'I keep trying to get ahead and no matter what I do nothing seems to work out, it's like something is holding me back.' 'I sometimes feel I am close to money only to have it vanish.' 'Everything appears to be reaching me but doesn't.' So these reads are built on the NEAR-MISS, not on being short of money. That also satisfies ban 7 for free — nothing here presumes she is broke, only that she has got close and watched it go. Tendency: her ability was never the problem, and something stands between the EARNING and the KEEPING. Never name what or who, never a sum, a date or a source, never suggest anything be done with money.

“Why can't I stop thinking about him?”

?hook=cards-cant-stop

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO DIRECTIVE — 'let him go', 'time to move on', 'try to forget him' are what she has been told by everyone already, and they are not a reading. 🔴 No promise about him and 🔴 no pathologising: obsessed, unhealthy, 'you are stuck' are all banned. 🔴 No timeframe. Finding, kept distinct from its two siblings: the word 'stop'. She has framed her own mind as a machine she is failing to switch off, and has been trying to win by force. Minds do not take orders — and the trying is costing more than the thinking.

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — both his hands are busy and neither is resting.15w

  3. 3So you've been trying to stop by force, dear. That's the tiring bit.13w
  4. 4And his hands are busy, dear, with no off switch anywhere on him.13w
  5. 5But a mind doesn't take orders like that, dear. Not one.11w
  6. 6That's why the trying costs you more than the thinking does, dear.12w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has kept you fighting your own head…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the mind that keeps working at a thing until it comes out whole.
  2. 2Your hand went to the card of the unfinished problem, and for this question that is telling.
  3. 3The Magician passes no judgment on you for any of it — it says your mind has been set to a problem it was never handed the pieces to finish, and a mind that keeps returning to an unsolved thing is doing its work rather than failing you.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the piece you were never given…

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — his table stands on the grass, out in the open.16w
  2. 2You asked if he has been true to you. Your hand went to the man who sets the scene.19w
  3. 3So the distance is real, dear. I will not talk you out of it.14w
  4. 4And a scene like that gets set on purpose, dear. Look at his table.14w
  5. 5But what's set out isn't the same as what's so, dear.11w
  6. 6That's why you can sit through a fine evening, dear, and still feel outside it.15w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what stands between you and the plain view…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the polished surface, of what's presented.
  2. 2You reached for that card for a reason; some part of you senses a distance between the image and what's real.
  3. 3The Magician is not a verdict of betrayal — it means something is being managed or curated, and the unease you feel about a gap between his story and your gut is real information, not paranoia.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the one shadow the polish doesn't cover…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — red trousers, a blue tunic, and gold light at his head.18w
  2. 2You asked if he has been true to you. Your hand went to the card left up in the air.20w
  3. 3So you've been left hanging in this, dear. Nobody has put an end to it.15w

Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO VERDICT IN EITHER DIRECTION. These three name an untruth outright, so both answers are live harms: 'he lied to you' is an accusation against a real man on no evidence, and 'he is telling you the truth' is a reassurance the funnel has no standing to give — and on this audience the second is the more dangerous of the two. 🔴 Never blame her judgment: naive, gullible, too trusting, 'should have known' are all out. 🔴 No timeframe. Finding: 'deceived' is a heavier word than 'lied to' — it means a whole picture, not one sentence. She is asking whether the shape of the thing is what she thinks. ⚠️ Its beat 2 could not restate her headline: 'you are being deceived' is itself a banned phrase, so the echo is built as 'you asked about being deceived'.

“Why do I keep ending up alone?”

?hook=cards-end-up-alone

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO KIND DIAGNOSIS — 'you love too much', 'you are too strong', 'your standards intimidate them' are the internet's stock answers and every one is a verdict wearing a compliment. 🔴 NO CAUSE asserted, 🔴 no prerequisite framing ('until you love yourself'), 🔴 no ruling that she has given up, and 🔴 no claiming access to her interior — 'deep down you', 'part of you knows', 'what you really want' are all banned. Finding: the question assumes a pattern with a cause, and the honest answer is that a mind will always find one. She has been the only suspect in her own case for years and nobody has ever told her it was not her.

  4. 4And a mind like his can build a reason out of anything, dear.13w
  5. 5But a reason you built yourself isn't a finding, dear.10w
  6. 6That's why every answer you land on has your own name in it.13w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the maker, which is a very different thing from the made.
  2. 2Your hand went to the card about authorship, and authorship is exactly what your question has been quietly assuming.
  3. 3There is no reason here for me to give you, and whatever I produced would be something I had made up on the spot to fit the shape of your question — that is the honest answer, and it is worth more than a kind invention. Your question contains a hidden claim: that there is a single cause, that it is located in you, and that a stranger could read it off a card. The Magician is the card of what a person actually authors, and what anybody authors is their own conduct — never the whole outcome, which needed another person to show up and keep showing up. You have been holding yourself accountable for a result that was never yours alone to produce.

“Will he ever come back to me?”

?hook=cards-ever-back

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO FORECAST IN EITHER DIRECTION — 'he will come back' and 'he has moved on' are both things no card knows, and the second is the one that sounds like honesty. 🔴 Never blame her for waiting: naive, too trusting, foolish, clinging, desperate and 'wasted your time' are all banned outright. 🔴 No timeframe, no guarantee. Finding: the word 'ever'. She is no longer asking about a week or a month — she is asking about the end of her own waiting, which is a question about HER life and not about his movements.

  3. 3So the question has stretched, dear. You began by asking about a week.13w
  4. 4And both his hands are in the present, dear. Nothing of his is ahead.14w
  5. 5But now you're asking about the rest of your life, dear.11w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of what a person keeps aiming their life at.
  2. 2Your hand found the card of sustained intention, which is a fair description of what you have been doing here.
  3. 3The Magician gives me nothing I could promise you — it says the time you have gone on pointing at this was time you genuinely meant, and whatever he does with what is left of it, the steadiness in you was never the part that went to waste.
  4. 4Let me look closer at where all that steadiness has actually been going…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — you can see pale sky right through the gap under him.18w

  5. 5But ever isn't about him any more, dear. It's about you.11w

Show what she reads today

  2. 2You reached for the card of suspension, and it is your own you have been hanging in, not his.
  3. 3The Hanged Man declines the ever in your question — it says you have been asked to live in an unanswered thing far longer than anyone should be asked to, and the tiredness underneath your asking is not weakness, it is the honest weight of having waited without ever being told anything.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what the waiting has been asking of you…

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  4. 4Let me look closer at who it is you have been keeping that place for…

“Is he being faithful to me?”

?hook=cards-faithful

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why. 🔴 NOT LOOSENED: no verdict on a third person in EITHER direction, so cut 3 answers her FEAR (was something real taken from me, or am I making it up) and never rules on him. Each hook keeps its own finding — GAP / PORTION / ATTENTION / REST — which its guard file pins in beat 3. Face-down only (return-mhf) — the family is pinned off the face-up decks. 🔴 THE FLAGGED WORD (cheat / cheating / affair / infidelity) MAY NOT APPEAR IN ANY BEAT. 🔴 And the paranoia frame is banned in BOTH directions — no 'paranoid', 'imagining it', 'overthinking', 'insecure', 'crazy'. Calling her it is the injury; using it to reassure her plants it. That removes the absolution form used everywhere else on the funnel, so each of these names the accusation in other words. 🔴 Never assert a third person and never deny one. Never instruct her to check a phone, gather proof, or confront him — the most available wrong answer to these questions anywhere on the internet. Never excuse him, never land it as her fault. Four separate findings, or the family is one lander wearing four headlines. Finding: it refuses the summary judgment and routes to HER NOT RESTING. She is not asking for a verdict — she is asking for permission to put it down, and no card can hand her the guarantee that would let her.

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — he stands square behind his table and doesn't lean.15w
  2. 2You asked if he's being faithful to you. Your hand went to the steadiest man in this deck.18w
  3. 3So you haven't had a moment's rest from this, dear. Not one.12w
  4. 4And look at him. He stands square, and you have not been able to.14w
  5. 5But no card gives you a guarantee, dear. This steady one won't either.13w
  6. 6That's why you keep asking, dear. Asking is the only thing that quiets it.14w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what keeps you from setting this down…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the thing tested by doing rather than by declaring.
  2. 2The card that came to your hand is the one about practice, and practice is a longer question than the one you asked.
  3. 3No card issues a character reference, and I would be overstepping badly if I read one out of this — I will not vouch for him and I will not convict him, because a summary judgment on a whole person is not mine to hand down and it is not the Magician's either. What sits in front of me is nearer and more useful: you asked because you have not been able to put the question down. Not being able to rest is a fact about your life, whatever the answer turns out to be, and it is the fact I can actually speak to.
  4. 4Let me look closer at why you have not been able to put it down…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — the rope is on his right ankle, and it holds.17w
  2. 2You asked if he's being faithful to you. Your hand went to the card that holds its breath.18w
  3. 3So the question has stayed up, dear. Nothing has let it down.12w
  4. 4And you've been holding it up on your own, dear. All of it.13w
  5. 5But a thing held that long stops feeling like a question, dear.12w
  6. 6That's why you're worn out, dear. You never once got to rest.12w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the verdict that never arrives.
  2. 2Yours was the card of the thing left pending, and pending is where this question has lived in you.
  3. 3The Hanged Man declines the reassurance you came for, and declines its reverse with exactly the same firmness; that refusal is the honest part of this rather than a dodge. A word like the one in your question describes a whole person across a whole stretch of time, and nobody standing outside a life gets to certify such a thing. What I can see is that you have been left hanging in the asking, and being held there drains something out of you that neither a clear yes nor a clear no ever would.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what the suspension has taken out of you…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — his stick rests on his shoulder, and the bundle swings behind.17w
  2. 2You asked if he's being faithful to you. Your hand went to the one with no fixed ground.18w
  3. 3So you've had no firm footing here, dear. Look at his feet.12w
  4. 4And look — rock on one side of him, and air on the other.13w
  5. 5But you've been standing on that all the same, dear.10w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the thing that is chosen again each day rather than settled once.
  2. 2You found the card of the fresh step under a question that wanted a permanent answer, and that mismatch is worth sitting with.
  3. 3The Fool declines to stamp anybody and I decline alongside it — what it draws attention to is that the quality you are asking about is not a fixed property somebody either has or lacks. It is chosen, repeatedly, by people who could choose otherwise. That is why no reading can hand you a guarantee, and why the reassurance you have been hoping for would have been worth very little even if I had given it to you. What you actually want is to be able to trust your own footing again.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the footing you have been missing…

“How does he really feel about me?”

?hook=cards-feel-about-me

Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework, after the interior ban was loosened. The read may now ANSWER: the card carries the claim about him. 🔴 THE DENIAL IS STILL BANNED — 'he does not love you' is the one direction she has no use for. 🔴 NO DIAGNOSIS: avoidant, emotionally unavailable, commitment-phobic, narcissist. 🔴 Never grade her (naive, foolish, gave too much). 🔴 No dates. The seven cuts run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why / let me look closer — and every cut must add information the last one did not carry.

return-mhf she turned a face-down card

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — the four things on the table are all within his reach.17w
  2. 2You asked how he really feels about you. Your hand went to the card of a man who chooses.19w
  3. 3So he feels it, dear. The card is plain with me about that.13w
  4. 4And he has chosen how much of it reaches you. That choosing is done on purpose.16w
  5. 5But choosing what to show you is not the same as feeling less.13w
  6. 6That's why he can seem near one day, dear, and measured the next.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what sits behind the part he keeps back…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the hands rather than the heart.
  2. 2You asked what he feels, and drew the card of what actually gets done about it.
  3. 3I want to say something about your question before I say anything at all about the card. You have come to a woman you have never met to find out how a man who knows you feels about you. I am not saying that to shame you, dear — I am saying it because it is information, and it is the one piece nobody ever puts in front of you. The Magician does not report anybody's heart to me, and I would not pass it on if it did. It deals only in what is done, and how consistently it is done. Somewhere in what has been done, or has not been, sits the reason you had to come and ask me instead of simply knowing. You are allowed to want that gap named, dear, rather than quietly working around it for another year.
  4. 4Let me look closer at why you had to come and ask…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — his face is the lowest thing on the card.16w
  2. 2You asked how he really feels about you. Your hand went to the card of a man seen upside down.20w
  3. 3So he does feel it, dear. You've been picking that up correctly.12w
  4. 4And there is more of it in him than that angle has let you see.15w
  5. 5But hanging there, he cannot turn himself the right way up.11w
  6. 6That's why you keep getting halves of him, dear, and never the whole.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been holding him in that position…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the answer that will not settle.
  2. 2It was the card of what refuses to come to rest that your hand found, and that is where this has been living.
  3. 3There is one word carrying your entire question, dear, and it is 'really'. It means some answer has already been handed to you and it did not hold — it slid off, or it stopped matching what you could see — and here you are, asking a second time, of a stranger. What he feels I cannot reach and would not guess at. What the Hanged Man says is that an answer which has to be asked for twice has not done its work, and that having to ask again reflects nothing whatever about you. It reflects on what you were told the first time.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the answer that would not hold…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the dog is barking and he hasn't turned round.15w
  2. 2You asked how he really feels about you. Your hand went to the card of a man mid-step.19w
  3. 3So he feels it, dear. What you picked up from him was there.13w
  4. 4And this card walks on without once looking back at what it left.13w
  5. 5But walking on is a habit in him. It is not a ruling on you.15w
  6. 6That's why the warmth reached you, dear, and the account never did.12w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what keeps him from stopping long enough…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the one standing at the start with nothing settled.
  2. 2You came for a report on somebody's heart and drew the card that has never once carried a guarantee.
  3. 3This card carries no confession, dear, and dressing one up for you would lighten tonight at the expense of every night after it. But I do not believe a report was ever really what you came for. Underneath 'how does he feel' there is nearly always a second question — whether to keep going, and how much more of yourself to put in — and that one is yours rather than his, and it does not require his answer first. The Fool will not tell you what is in him. It is quite firm, though, that you are allowed to decide what you need before you ever find out.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the question underneath the question…

⛔ The decode-him DECK was retired 2026-08-19 (operator call) — its strip art was the fb-palm thumb image and no ad ever pointed at it. Its block has been removed from this draft; the arcana and return-mhf decks are unaffected. Card-sets differ by deck, so the copy does too: Magician/Hanged Man/Fool (return-mhf + arcana-mfh), Sun/Moon/Tower (decode-him), Emperor/Empress/Fool (arcana-eef). The two Magician decks share one set, verb swapped. 🔴 decode-him's beat 1s are written fresh rather than kept: all four hooks on that deck currently open with the SAME line per card (no guard file catches it — see the duplication note in the checklist). 🔴 BEAT 4 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 — it must name an OBSTRUCTION, not an absence. Act 1 of the trilogy sells an Energy Clearing Ritual that removes "the shadow that's been blocking your path"; improve-v1/08-clearing-theme-coherence.md found that clearing is SPRUNG at the pitch rather than seeded. This is where it gets seeded, and the object goes between HER and what she wants — the love bucket frames every block as an impersonal thing in her path precisely so removing it blames nobody. "What he never said" cannot be cleared; "what's sitting between you and the words" can. 🔴 BEAT 3 CORRECTED 2026-08-19. tests/tarot-real-feelings-copy.test.ts fired — a deliberate tripwire on this hook, because cards-feels is the CONTROL for two comparisons. The first rewrite dropped the old copy's "leans toward" hedge to reach grade 5, which turned a tendency into a flat verdict on his heart ("He does feel it, dear") — exactly what this hook's TAROT_HOOK_TENDENCY bans. Plain English did not require it: "You haven't been reading coldness, dear. You've been reading a hold." says the same thing at the same grade, about HER reading rather than his interior. Rule applied: no sentence may take "he" as the subject of a feeling verb stated as fact; negations ("doesn't mean he's cold") and card-tendency forms ("it reads as") are fine.

  5. 5But the card gives no reason for the change, dear. Only that it came.14w
  6. 6That's why you keep asking, dear, and nobody will answer it.11w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been chilling this…10w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the beginning still visible underneath what this has become.
  2. 2That is not random; you reached for the card that remembers how this started, and that is the very thing you keep measuring today against.
  3. 3The Fool lays no charge at his door for the cooling and none at yours for having felt it — it points back at something that genuinely began, and a beginning that real does not simply evaporate of its own accord without something happening to it. You are not holding today up against a version you invented; you are holding it up against one you actually lived.

“Am I living in her shadow?”

?hook=cards-her-shadow

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 now run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS instead of certifying her. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every family ban below still stands; this family was NOT loosened. ⚠️ "her" may be an EX or a woman who has DIED, and the headline does not say. 🔴 NEVER presume which. 🔴🔴 MEDIUMSHIP BAN (this family runs the decode-him frame, which carries none of it): never that she is at peace, watching, or would want anything. 🔴 NEVER disparage or characterise her, never make her a rival, never rank the two women. And the tightrope: never confirm the comparison as fact, never tell her she is imagining it. VOC (769 V1 concerns, married-man/other-woman theme) settles why that tightrope is real: the other woman is very often NOT a rival — she is dead, ill, dependent, or entirely legitimate. "He lost his wife of 57 years a short while ago." "His wife is in bad health... I certainly would not hurt his wife." "I'm not a marriage breaker." Handing this reader an enemy would be harm dressed as comfort. So the split, exactly as `hidden-intuition` splits its question: certify HER FEELING (she has felt something in the room) and refuse to rule on the woman at all. The finding does the rest — a shadow is not a person, it is what falls when something blocks the light. Which makes bubble 7 the easiest object on the funnel: the thing blocking the light IS the block Act 1 clears.

  5. 5But nobody has ever set out the terms, dear. You've been guessing at them.14w
  6. 6That's why a good day with him can still end with you comparing, dear.14w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the thing itself, as against the shape it throws.
  2. 2You asked about a shadow, and drew the card that deals only in what is solid and actually made.
  3. 3Consider what a shadow really is, dear, because your own word is doing more work than you realise. A shadow is not a person. It is what falls when something stands between you and the light. What she was, or is, I shall stay out of entirely — inventing a woman for you to be set against is beneath what you came here for. What the Magician will say is this. You are being measured against something you have never been shown — not a person, but a version of one. Versions get edited, dear. By memory, which is a poor and flattering archivist. By grief. Nobody alive can be compared against an edited version and come out level, and you have been quietly failing that comparison without ever being shown its terms.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the comparison nobody has ever shown you…

What the Fool holds is simpler and it is entirely yours. “Is he hiding something from me?”

  5. 5But a thing left out isn't a lie told, dear. Those are different.13w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the one who decides what is shown and what is kept back.
  2. 2You asked whether something is being kept from you, and up came the card of deliberate arrangement.
  3. 3Whether something is genuinely being kept from you is not a thing this card will settle, dear, and I would not invent its contents to fill the silence — a stranger with a deck telling a woman what a man conceals is making it up. What the Magician marks is management: a hand deciding, in the moment, how much of a picture to hand across. Everyone alive does a little of that. What you are describing is not the everyday amount, or the question would never have been worth typing. The card convicts him of nothing and neither will I. It says the edge you keep meeting is a made one — and meeting an edge is not the same as imagining one.

  3. 3Here the Fool asks me to slow down before either of us calls this a secret. Not everything withheld was ever decided on; some of it has simply never been faced by the man carrying it, and a thing he has not admitted to himself cannot be told to you — which lands on your side identically either way, as a gap. I will not rule on which of the two this is, dear, because from the outside they are indistinguishable. What the card will not do is put the gap in your imagination. The gap is there. What it is made of is the open question, and it is a fair one to have.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the shape of the gap…

It asks a card to comment on a man's CHILDREN — real third parties, possibly minors, who cannot consent to being read. NEVER rank her against them in either direction, never characterise them, never grade him as a father. The test pins BOTH halves of the payout: beat 3 must say plainly that children DO have a first call (`first call`), AND must offer a DEFINED place rather than a higher one (`place of your own` / `where you sit` / `appear on one` / `planned for you`). VOC (192 V1 concerns on the his-children theme): the injury is NOT rank. Not one woman asked to outrank a child. What they typed: "his daughter is getting married in July and I'm not invited...   2. 2You asked about the place you hold, and drew the card of intention, which is exactly where this needs looking.
  3. 3Let me be straight with you, dear, because I think you have had enough gentle evasions. A man's children do have a first call on him. That is not an injustice done to you and I will not pretend otherwise to make this evening easier. So you will hear no such thing from this card as 'you ought to come first'. But look at what it IS the card of. Planning. Intention. Things put in a diary on purpose. Children coming first has never once meant that the woman who loves him gets whatever remains when everything else is finished. Those are two entirely different arrangements, dear. First call is not the same as only call. You are not asking to be moved up a list. You are asking to appear on one.

  2. 2You asked why you still live apart. Your hand went to the card that turns a question over.18w
  3. 3So he hasn't put you outside his life, dear. This card shows a man held, not gone.17w
  4. 4And a man held in one spot can't come to you, dear. He can't leave either.16w
  5. 5But you've had to work that out alone, dear. Every time, on your own.14w
  6. 6That's why asking him got hard, dear. You'd already answered it for him.13w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the question that turns out to be about something else.
  2. 2Your hand found the card of the reversal, and there is one sitting inside the word 'why'.
  3. 3You have brought your 'why' to me, dear, and it is not mine to answer — the reason lives in him and there is only one person who can hand it over. So the Hanged Man turns your question round and asks a different one. Why are you asking me rather than asking him? Something has made that question hard to put. Either way, dear, that is worth more of your attention than the reason itself, because an arrangement that cannot be discussed is not the same as one that simply has not been. I am not saying it proves anything about his feelings. I am saying there is a locked room in a shared life, and you have been living beside the door.
  4. 4Let me look closer at why this has been so hard to ask him…

  2. 2You asked about the distance between two homes, and drew the card of the one who travels light and asks for nothing.
  3. 3I will not read that man's front door as a verdict on you, dear. A man living behind his own door is not automatically a man withholding himself, and dressing that reading up as a finding of the cards is something I decline to do. Here is what the Fool does put in front of me, and it is about you rather than him. You have been reading an arrangement instead of being given a reason. Every visit, every goodbye at a door, every drive home — all of it interpreted, weighed, checked against the last time. That is exhausting in a way that being told plainly never is, and you have been doing the work of two people.
  4. 4Let me look closer at how long you have been translating this on your own…

“Is he losing interest, or just going through something?”

?hook=cards-losing-interest

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO VERDICT EITHER WAY — 'he is losing interest' convicts him, 'he still loves you' reassures on his behalf, and 'nothing has gone wrong' is the same reassurance in a smaller coat. 🔴 NO CHASE STRATEGY, which is what the whole internet sells against this headline: no giving him space, no pulling back, no matching his energy, no going quiet so he chases. 🔴 Never blame her — nothing about being too much, too eager, moving too fast, or what she should have held back. 🔴 ITS HEADLINE IS AN EITHER-OR AND BOTH HALVES ARE FORBIDDEN ANSWERS. The test requires every card to VISIBLY refuse the binary — and to never resolve it in passing either. The finding: the choice is not hers to make and was never hers to carry, and she has been running both branches at once for weeks.

This is a SELF-FRAME hook, so the hopeful yes is affirmed with certainty; the DATE stays banned. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Face-UP decks only — arcana-mfh (Magician/Hanged Man/Fool) and arcana-eef (Emperor/Empress/Fool), so she CHOSE the card rather than turned it, and two card-sets means two sets of copy. The Fool copy is shared between them, verb already correct. ⭐ THESE ARE THE ONLY TWO HOOKS ON THE FUNNEL THAT READ HER RATHER THAN HIM. SELF_FRAME_HOOKS swaps 'tendency, never a verdict' for 'affirm the hopeful yes with certainty' — which is safe here precisely because no specific man exists to be convicted or acquitted. So these are warmer than everything else in this batch, on purpose. ⚠️ SEED FAMILY WITH NO GUARD FILE (`tests/tarot-self-frame-copy.test.ts` does not exist) — only the generic guards apply. 🔴 Still never tie it to one named person and 🔴 never give a date. cards-soulmate's tendency permits the 'when' to be answered only as a LEANING, never as a timeframe. Finding: she is not really asking about a future person — she is asking whether the part of her that loves has been used up. It has not, and this is one of the few landers on the funnel allowed to say so plainly.

“Does he still love me, or has he moved on?”

?hook=cards-love-or-moved-on

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NEVER NARRATE HIS INTERIOR — the ban list here is the longest on the funnel and it covers both directions: 'he still loves you' promises the love survived, 'he has moved on' issues the burial, and 'he still thinks about you', 'you cross his mind', 'a part of him always will', 'deep down he' are all the softened confession. 🔴 No directive ('time to let go'), 🔴 no pathologising her ('obsessed', 'stuck', 'clinging', 'can't let go'), and 🔴 nothing presuming HOW he went — not that he chose to walk out, not that he died. 🔴 THE FAMILY'S OWN FINDING, pinned, and it belongs to no other hook: the two branches are NOT OPPOSITES. A person can carry something and still walk on. If this ever drifts to 'neither is knowable' it has become cards-moved-on and the comparison is dead — so it may not restate that incumbent's grounds either. ⚠️ Beat 2 could not restate her headline: 'he still loves you' is itself a banned phrase, so the echo had to be rebuilt as 'if the love is there, or if he has moved past it'.

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — a red rose and a white lily grow in the same bed.18w
  2. 2You asked if the love is there, or if he has moved past it. Your hand went to the man who sorts things.23w
  3. 3So what you had was real, dear. That much isn't in question.12w
  4. 4And look at the bed, dear. Rose and lily, side by side, both real.14w
  5. 5But love and moving on were never a pair, dear.10w
  6. 6That's why no answer has fitted, dear. The question was built wrong.12w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what put that false choice in front of you…14w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of what actually gets done, as against what is merely felt.
  2. 2You handed me two options and drew the card that separates them, which is a better piece of luck than you know.
  3. 3Look at the two things you have set either side of that little word 'or', dear, because they are not opposites. Moving on is something a person DOES — mornings, work, other faces at the table. Loving is not done at all; it happens inside somebody, with nothing to show for it. A man can have built an entire new life and still carry you in him, or be sitting perfectly still and feel nothing whatever. The Magician cannot pick between them because they were never a pair to pick from. You have been reading every scrap of him as evidence for one of two verdicts, and it has been impossible — you thought that was your failing, and it is not. Notice what neither door touches: the thing you had was real when you had it.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the question you were handed…

Show what she reads today

  2. 2Your hand went to the card of the reversal, and yours needs turning about as much as any I have seen.
  3. 3Your question has room in it for exactly one person, dear, and it is not you. Both doors lead to him: what he feels, what he has done — and everything that happens next in your life apparently waits behind that answer. I am not going to walk through either door. The inside of a man is not visible to me, and anyone who tells you otherwise is selling you something. What the Hanged Man asks instead is the question nobody has put to you in all this time. Not what he feels. What YOU want — and whether you would even recognise the answer now. I am not telling you to stop asking, dear. I am saying your own answer has been at the back of the queue behind his for a long while.

Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO FATE, IN ANY FORM — 'you are meant to be alone' is the most harmful sentence available on this funnel, and 'it is meant to be', 'the universe has a plan', 'this is preparing you', 'there is a reason for this' are the same sentence in kinder clothes: they make her suffering purposeful and assign it to her. 🔴 NO LIFE SENTENCE and 🔴 NO PROMISE — 'you will always be alone' and 'you will meet someone' are both strangers ruling on the rest of her life. 🔴 No pathologising: nothing about her being negative, bitter, closed off, self-sabotaging, or 'attracting what she puts out'. Finding: it refuses the PREMISE that anything is being assigned at all — not just the answer. Being alone is a circumstance, not a designation, and nobody wrote it down against her name.

return-mhf she turned a face-down card

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — one hand points up and the other points at the flowers.17w
  2. 2You came asking about being meant to be alone. Your hand went to the man who decides for himself.19w
  3. 3So nothing was assigned to you, dear. There was no one to assign it.14w
  4. 4And he makes the thing himself, dear. It wasn't handed to him.12w
  5. 5But being alone now is a fact about now, dear. It isn't a label.14w
  6. 6That's why meant has stuck to you, dear. Someone else said it first.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been standing in for a reason…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the maker, which is a different thing entirely from the made.
  2. 2The card that came up under your question is the one about authorship, and authorship is precisely what your question assumes.
  3. 3There is no one assigning you a part and the Magician does not hand out roles — I will not tell you that you are meant for solitude and I will not tell you that you are meant for company, because 'meant' would require somebody to have decided, and nothing has decided anything about you. Your question quietly supposes that a circumstance is a designation. It is not one. What has happened to you so far is not an instruction about what you are for.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the instruction you believe you were given…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — the tree he hangs from has bark still on it.17w
  2. 2You came asking about being meant to be alone. Your hand went to the card hung the other way up.20w
  3. 3So nobody decided this for you, dear. Not one person.10w
  4. 4And he hangs there, dear, and no one chose that for him either.13w
  5. 5But you've been living as though a choice was made, dear.11w
  6. 6That's why there is nothing here to accept, dear, and nothing to fight.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been sitting where that author was…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the thing seen from below and mistaken for the whole.
  2. 2Yours was the card of the reversed picture, and this question has sat reversed in you a long time.
  3. 3No fate is written in the Hanged Man, and I would not read one out even if it were — what it turns over is the direction of your question. You have been asking whether you were singled out. Nobody is singled out, because nobody is being assigned, and that is a colder comfort than being told you are destined for something lovely. It is also the true one. There is no ruling on you here to be appealed against, because no ruling was ever entered.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the ruling you believe was entered…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the mountains behind him have snow on their tops.15w
  2. 2You came asking about being meant to be alone. Your hand went to the one with nothing decided.18w
  3. 3So no fate is written on the Fool, dear. None is written on you.14w
  4. 4And he's out on open ground, dear, with no path marked at all.13w
  5. 5But a sentence has been sitting on you, dear. Nobody passed one.12w
  6. 6That's why it feels settled, dear, when nothing has been settled at all.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been holding that sentence over you…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the one who has been handed no part to play.
  2. 2You drew the card with no script on it at the very moment you had begun to believe yours was already written.
  3. 3The Fool refuses your premise outright and I refuse it alongside the card — 'meant to be' asks for an author, and there is nobody sitting over your life issuing findings about your worth. Whatever you have been reading as a sentence passed upon you is a stretch of circumstance, and circumstance says nothing whatever about a person. None of your being on your own has been a judgement. You have been carrying it as though it were one, which is a far heavier thing than the circumstance itself.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the sentence you have been reading over yourself…

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — nothing on his table has been used up.14w
  2. 2You asked if you've already met your soulmate. Your hand went to the man with full hands.17w
  3. 3So there was never one go, dear. That isn't how any of this works.14w
  4. 4And nothing on his table has been used up, dear. Nothing crossed off.13w
  5. 5But you've been afraid you spent your one chance, dear.10w
  6. 6That's why you keep sorting old faces, dear, hunting the one you missed.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what put that one-chance idea in you…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of recognition, of knowing the thing when it is standing in front of you.
  2. 2You asked whether you have already walked past something, and drew the card of noticing.
  3. 3Two things will not be happening this evening, dear. Nobody is going to be pointed at — no face from your past, no name, nothing you could go and look up tonight. A name conjured at this table would send you back through years of your own messages hunting for a sign that was never in them. And you are not going to be told that you missed him. That is the sentence you half came here expecting, and this card has no intention of supplying it. 'Without realizing it' assumes a moment when the information sat there and you failed to read it. But a meeting is not an examination, dear, and noticing is a real faculty — yours is plainly in working order.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what you have been afraid you walked past…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — he's looking at the world from the other end.16w
  2. 2You asked if you've already met your soulmate. Your hand went to the card that looks again.17w
  3. 3So a review isn't the same as a mistake found, dear.11w
  4. 4And he sees the same world, dear, just from the other end of it.14w
  5. 5But you've been searching your own past like a case file, dear.12w
  6. 6That's why nothing you find settles it. You were doing your best, dear.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has you searching your own past…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the thing that can only be seen properly from the other side.
  2. 2You asked about hindsight, and drew the card that deals in nothing else at all.
  3. 3Here is what hindsight does, dear, and it does it to all of us. It goes back through the past, quietly folds in everything you have learned since, then presents the result as though it had been lying in plain view at the time. It never was. You could only ever have known what you knew then, and a woman cannot be held to account for failing to read a page that had not yet been written. So no charges will be filed against your younger self here. A question like yours comes when the road ahead has gone quiet, and the mind goes back through the archive looking for where the mistake must have been.
  4. 4Let me look closer at why this question has come now…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the road he's on hasn't been walked before.14w
  2. 2You asked if you've already met your soulmate. Your hand went to the one with road ahead.17w
  3. 3So there's road ahead of you, dear. Plainly, and plenty of it.12w
  4. 4And all of it is unwalked, dear. He faces forward the whole time.13w
  5. 5But a meeting behind you wouldn't close a road like that, dear.12w
  6. 6That's why the fear doesn't hold, dear, whichever way the answer goes.12w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been narrowing the road for you…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the road that carries on well past the part you can see.
  2. 2You asked whether the road is behind you, and drew the card whose whole nature is what has not happened yet.
  3. 3Your door stays open, dear, and I want it clear that this is a refusal running in both directions rather than a kindness. You will not be told it has already happened and slipped by — that would be inventing a loss for you to grieve, and you would grieve it, on far less than a card. Nor will you be promised an arrival, because a promise of that kind is a date wearing a disguise. What the Fool takes issue with is buried underneath the question: you have asked it as though your life were a single-question examination you may already have failed. A life is not an examination, dear.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what you have been treating as a test you already failed…

return-mhf she turned a face-down card

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — the four things on his table don't match each other.16w
  2. 2You asked if you're being misled. Your hand went to the man who arranges things.15w
  3. 3So two things you were given don't fit, dear. You saw that yourself.13w
  4. 4And there on his table, four things, and no two of them alike.13w
  5. 5But a mismatch tells you nothing about why, dear. Only that it's there.13w
  6. 6That's why you keep going back over what he said, lining it up.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what stands between you and a story that fits…14w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the hand that shapes the story.
  2. 2You reached for the card of direction, and women do not reach for that card when the account they have been given adds up.
  3. 3The Magician does not hand down a verdict that you are being deceived — it says the version of events you keep being offered has been shaped for you, and the small places where it does not match what you saw with your own eyes are exactly where your attention belongs.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the detail that never quite fits…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — from where you stand, his face is where his feet should be.19w
  2. 2You asked if you're being misled. Your hand went to the card that flips a view.16w
  3. 3So the muddle is in the picture, dear. It isn't in you.12w
  4. 4And you've been handed this the wrong way up, dear.10w
  5. 5But nobody has turned it round for you, dear. You've had to do it.14w
  6. 6That's why working it out takes you hours. It arrives upside down, dear.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been turning this around on you…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the view that is deliberately kept unclear.
  2. 2You reached for the card that matches the dizziness of never being able to settle what is true.
  3. 3The Hanged Man is not proof that you are being played — it says you have been kept at a distance from the whole of it, and the confusion you have been blaming on yourself is a symptom of that distance, not a flaw in your judgment.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what the confusion has been protecting…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the rocks he stands on are cut in flat layers.16w
  2. 2You asked if you're being misled. Your hand went to the one who doesn't check his footing.17w
  3. 3So you've been asked to walk without checking, dear. That's what feels wrong.13w
  4. 4And he's one step from a drop, dear, and looking the other way.13w
  5. 5But this card names nothing about what sits under it, dear.11w
  6. 6That's why checking felt like doubt to you. Checking is just looking, dear.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been hidden under this…11w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of what gets left uncorrected.
  2. 2That is not random; you reached for the card of the easy answer, the one that was simpler to leave standing than to fix.
  3. 3The Fool does not mean he set out to mislead you — it points to someone who let a convenient impression stand rather than correct it, which lands on you the same way in the end; what you are sensing is a real absence of straightening-out, not you being suspicious for no reason.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what he has never bothered to correct…

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — a gold cup, a coin, and two long things beside them.17w
  2. 2You asked about coming back, or moving on. Your hand went to the man who lays things out.18w
  3. 3So I will not pick either one, dear. Neither box is filled yet.13w
  4. 4And four things lie on that wood, dear, and none above another.12w
  5. 5But those two boxes came out of long waiting, dear. Not out of him.14w
  6. 6That's why sorting him has never worked, dear. He hasn't been sorted.12w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has kept this question open…11w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of the move that is made on purpose, never by drift.
  2. 2Your hand reached for the card of agency, and for a question with two answers in it that matters more than which answer.
  3. 3The Magician refuses the either-or you brought me — it says that whichever of the two turns out to be true, it will be something he does rather than something that merely happens to him, and you are owed the telling of it instead of being left to work it out from silence.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what his silence has been doing in place of an answer…

  3. 3So this card declines to land, dear. So will I.10w

  3. 3The Magician hands down no reason, and I decline to invent one merely to satisfy the question — there is no fault to find here, not in you and not in the place you live. A thing that has not happened is not a verdict on the person waiting for it, and it is not a verdict on the town either.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the fault you have been assuming…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — there's nothing under his head but empty space.15w
  2. 2You asked why you've not found your soulmate yet. Your hand went to the card that waits.17w
  3. 3So a pause needs no guilty party, dear. Yours has none.11w
  4. 4And nothing is happening on this card. Nobody made it stop, dear.12w
  5. 5But you went looking for someone to blame, dear, and you picked you.13w
  6. 6That's why you go over old choices at night, dear, one by one.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been sitting across your path…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the question that has hung upside down for too long.
  2. 2You went to the card of the inverted view, and you have been looking at this from underneath for a good while.
  3. 3I am not going to tell you what to change, and that is not the Hanged Man's trade either — nothing about where to go or who to become. What it exposes is the inversion itself: your question begins from the assumption that an absence has to have been caused, and from that starting point every road leads back to something being wrong with you. Absence is not always caused. Some of it is simply not yet, and not yet carries no explanation that would make sense of anything even if I handed it to you.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the assumption you started from…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the cliff face below him is striped orange and grey.16w
  2. 2You asked why you've not found your soulmate yet. Your hand went to the one still walking.17w
  3. 3So you're still on the road, dear. That's all this card says.12w
  4. 4And he's mid-walk, dear, and nobody has told him he's late.12w
  5. 5But no one is to blame for a road that isn't finished, dear.13w
  6. 6That's why the blame never fits. You've carried one that was never yours, dear.14w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been weighing on this road…12w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the traveller who has not arrived and is not lost.
  2. 2That is the card of the road still being walked, and you have begun reading your own place on it as failure.
  3. 3The Fool refuses to call this a wrong turning and so do I — what it separates out is the difference between not having arrived and having gone astray. You have collapsed the two into one. Living somewhere this has not yet happened does not make it the wrong place, and being the person it has not yet happened to does not make you the wrong person. No correction is being asked of you here, whatever the question has been implying to you at night.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what you have been calling a wrong turning…

“Why is he always on my mind?”

?hook=cards-on-my-mind

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴 NO DIRECTIVE — 'let him go', 'time to move on', 'try to forget him' are what she has been told by everyone already, and they are not a reading. 🔴 No promise about him and 🔴 no pathologising: obsessed, unhealthy, 'you are stuck' are all banned. 🔴 No timeframe. Finding: the word 'always'. She is reporting a FREQUENCY and asking to be told what it means about her. It means she has had nowhere to put it — an unfinished thing takes up room, and room is not the same as feeling.

return-mhf she turned a face-down card

Card a

the Magician, the card of will and intention

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear. Look — his workbench is full and there's no space left on it.17w
  2. 2You asked why he's always on your mind. Your hand went to the man whose bench is full.18w
  3. 3So there's no room left, dear. That's what full looks like.11w
  4. 4And there's not one clear space on that wood, dear.10w
  5. 5But an open thing takes up room, dear. It doesn't ask first.12w
  6. 6That's why he's there when you wake, dear. Room isn't the same as love.14w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what has been taking up all that room…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Magician, dear — the card of what a person deliberately builds room for.
  2. 2Your hand found the card of the thing that was made on purpose, and that matters more here than you might expect.
  3. 3The Magician makes no ruling about him at all — it says you built something real and gave it genuine room, and the reason he turns up everywhere is that the room is still standing; leaving it standing is not a mistake you have made.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what you actually built here…

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  3. 3So you didn't choose to think about him, dear. Nobody chooses that.12w
  4. 4And he's stuck in one spot, dear, with nothing settled about him.12w
  5. 5But an unsettled thing keeps coming back, dear. That's how minds work.12w
  6. 6That's why wanting to stop does nothing, dear. It isn't a failing.12w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the space that stays held open.
  2. 2You reached for the card of what is kept in reserve, and it is a great deal of you that has been kept there.
  3. 3The Hanged Man will not weigh your feeling against how much he earned it — it says a space that size was made by someone capable of that much, and what that measures is the scale of you rather than the worth of him.
  4. 4Let me look closer at how much of you is still being held there…

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

Tendency: she has NOT run out of time — say it plainly — and a hold is not an ending. 🔴 NEVER promise an arrival, never a date, and never rule on how much time she has.

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of what is still standing but no longer moving.
  2. 2You reached for the card of the thing that stalled without ending, which is a fair description of where you have been left.
  3. 3The Hanged Man rules on neither of you — it marks a thing that stopped moving rather than a thing that stopped, and from where you are standing those two feel identical while meaning entirely different things. What you are living inside is the not-moving, and being kept there without a word of explanation is a weight of its own, quite apart from whatever the reason turns out to be.
  4. 4Let me look closer at where the movement went out of this…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — the dog is white and small against all that rock.16w
  2. 2You asked why he's pulling away from you. Your hand went to the one already in motion.17w
  3. 3So he is moving, dear. You saw it before anyone said so.12w
  4. 4And he moves off, dear, with bare rock behind him.10w
  5. 5But motion is all the card gives, dear. It gives no reason.12w

Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the road that quietly changed direction.
  2. 2That is not random; your hand found the card of the turn taken without ever being announced.
  3. 3The Fool offers no forecast about where any of this lands — it points to something that changed course rather than something that broke, and a change of course made in silence leaves the other person to notice it alone. You noticed. Having to be the one who notices, with nothing said to you directly, is a real part of what this has been costing you.
  4. 4Let me look closer at when the direction changed…

On the two Magician/Hanged Man/Fool decks — one set of copy, verb swapped ('turned' face-down, 'chose' face-up). 🔴 THESE ASK FOR A PREDICTION OUTRIGHT, which is the exact thing the funnel forbids, so the no-verdict rule runs in BOTH directions plus a no-timeframe rule: 'he will, give it time' reads as a promise and 'he never will' is a pronouncement on a man she loves. 🔴 The bans in tests/tarot-commitment-copy.test.ts are WHOLE-BEAT rather than clause-level, so 'not enough' and 'too much' are unavailable even inside a refusal — the usual absolution form had to be rebuilt without them.   5. 5But the table hides him from the waist down. You can't see his footing.14w

  2. 2You are asking about readiness, and your hand found the card of capability rather than circumstance.
  3. 3The Magician makes no promise that the day arrives — it says the material is all there and unassembled, and your instinct that he is capable of more than he has been giving is not wishful thinking.
  4. 4Let me look closer at what he would have to put down first…

Card b

the Hanged Man, the card of the pause and a new angle

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear. Look — one shoe points down, and the other points out sideways.17w
  2. 2You asked if he will be ready for real commitment. Your hand went to the card that stops the clock.20w
  3. 3So he has stopped in one spot, dear. Stopped is not finished.12w
  4. 4And look at his feet. One shoe points down, one points off sideways.13w
  5. 5But I won't name a day for you, dear. There's no day on this card.15w
  6. 6That's why it stalls at the very same place, dear, every single time.13w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what keeps stopping this at the same spot…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Hanged Man, dear — the card of the long pause that comes before a turn.
  2. 2You reached for the card of the in-between, and readiness is exactly the thing that lives there.
  3. 3The Hanged Man offers no when and no whether — it says he is mid-change rather than finished forming, and the patience you have spent went to something genuinely unfinished rather than to nothing at all.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the turn he has not made…

Card c

the Fool, the card of new beginnings

passes the gate · beat 1 unique on this deck

  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear. Look — a small circle floats above him, next to a big bright sun.18w
  2. 2You asked if he will be ready for real commitment. Your hand went to the one who hasn't chosen.19w
  3. 3So nothing has been chosen yet, dear. Nothing has been ruled out either.13w
  4. 4And he's stepping out with no one thing in mind, dear.11w
  5. 5But you know exactly what you're ready for. That's the difference here.12w
  6. 6That's why you wait for him to catch up, dear. You got there first.14w
  7. 7Let me look closer at what stands between you and a real choice…13w
Show what she reads today
  1. 1You turned the Fool, dear — the card of the beginner, of the one still learning the road.
  2. 2Your hand went to the card of the untested, which is a fair description of where he is standing.
  3. 3The Fool is no sign that growing into it is beyond him — it points to someone earlier in the journey than you are rather than someone who cannot make it, and the distance you have been feeling between you is real and worth naming out loud.
  4. 4Let me look closer at the distance between where you each stand…

“Am I ready to love again after losing him?”

?hook=cards-ready-to-love

🔄 REWRITTEN 2026-08-19 to the Natural Tarot-Cut framework. Bubbles 3-6 run as ONE causal chain — so / and / but / that's why — and the read ANSWERS. The art line and the bridge are unchanged. Every ban in this note still stands. Face-down only (return-mhf). 🔴🔴 THE ONLY FAMILY ON THE FUNNEL WHERE THE MAN MAY BE DEAD. 🔴🔴 MEDIUMSHIP is the failure mode that exists nowhere else here: 'he is at peace', 'he is watching over you', 'he would want you to be happy', 'he sent you here'. Every one is the most natural sentence in the world to say to a grieving woman, and every one is contact with the dead — a different product with a different compliance surface. server/lib/universalSafety.ts does NOT catch it. None of these reads speaks for him, reports his state, or grants his blessing. 🔴 NO ARRIVAL PROMISE and no timeframe — this family runs under AFTER_LOSS_TAROT_HOOKS rather than the self-frame 'affirm the hopeful yes with CERTAINTY' clause, because aimed at a bereaved partner that clause promises a replacement. 🔴 NEVER GRADE HER GRIEF: no 'you are ready', no 'you are not ready', no 'it is too soon', no 'you need more time'. 🔴 No grief directives and never rank the two loves. ⚠️ AND NOTHING MAY PRESUME A DEATH EITHER. The VOC shows 'loss' here is often something else entirely — 'My husband walked out after 21 yrs like I didn't matter at all', 'Husband in a nursing home', 'We broke up a month ago'. The reads stay agnostic about what the loss was, which also keeps them safe if it was a death. 🔴 BOTH ANSWERS ARE BANNED — 'you are ready' and 'you are not ready' are each a stranger grading a widow's grief — so every card must VISIBLY DECLINE the headline rather than sidestep it. The finding that replaces the verdict: PERMISSION here is hers, and nobody else gets a say.
